# Verification of ZeroG.md against WebKit 31a83a39

Generated by `tools/verification_report.py` from the verification workflow: one agent per area checked every claim of [ZeroG.md](../../ZeroG.md) against the WebKit checkout pinned in `WEBKIT_REVISION` (main, 2026-10-28); an independent skeptic re-checked every claim judged REFUTED or PARTIAL and tried to overturn it. **Final** is the skeptic's verdict when one exists.

Claims: 207 — PARTIAL 99, CONFIRMED 92, REFUTED 12, UNVERIFIABLE 4. Verdicts changed by the skeptic: 16.

## Summary

### Already resolved

| Finding | Resolution |
| --- | --- |
| `USE_SYSTEM_MALLOC` is a hard `#error` on Windows | Vendored mimalloc on Windows, system malloc on Linux (`OptionsZeroG.cmake`) |
| `/MT` is not reachable with `-D` (OptionsMSVC forces `MultiThreadedDLL`) | `OptionsZeroG.cmake` re-sets it after `include(OptionsMSVC)`; dependencies built `/MT` |
| A new `PORT` needs `ALL_PORTS` plus a platform branch, or Windows becomes `PLATFORM(WIN)` | Patch 0002: `PLATFORM(ZEROG)` |
| Static `libJavaScriptCore.a` lacks the `JavaScriptCoreJIT` objects; thin archives | `tools/build.py bundle`, `USE_THIN_ARCHIVES=OFF` |
| Code outside WebKit must define `STATICALLY_LINKED_WITH_*` or `JS_NO_EXPORT` | Patch 0003 removes export decorations; `JS_NO_EXPORT` everywhere |
| zig's libc++ cannot be mixed with libstdc++ / MSVC STL objects | Linux builds everything with zig; Windows keeps clang-cl (MSVC STL); zig only links |
| **WebCore's CJK codecs build their tables from ICU converters at runtime** (EUC-JP, Big-5, windows-949, gb18030; `EncodingTables.cpp`), with a `RELEASE_ASSERT` on the result; EUC-TW is declared with `DECLARE_ENCODING_NAME_NO_ALIASES` | The ICU data filter had dropped them (first CJK page would crash): `tools/icu_data.py` now keeps every converter WebCore opens by name or declares, matched the way ICU compares names; the link test opens all of them. Minimal ICU data 12.6 -> 13.7 MB |

### Findings that reshape phase 1 (WebCore)

1. **The embedding layer must be written from scratch.** Ultralight's public WebCore fork does not contain its `Page`/`ChromeClient`/`LocalFrameLoaderClient`/`EditorClient`; those are in its closed module. References: WebKitLegacy (Apple-only, but the client implementations) and WebCore's `EmptyClients`. The Empty* clients are mostly `final`, so they are templates to copy, not base classes.
2. **Networking is WebKit2-shaped.** `ResourceHandle` is a stub for curl, `NetworkStorageSession` moved to `Source/WebKit`, `CookieJar` asserts outside Cocoa, cookie header handling lives in `NetworkDataTaskCurl`. ZeroG needs its own LoaderStrategy (or a revived `ResourceHandleCurl` over `CurlRequest`), cookie jar subclass and storage session. `CurlSSLHandle::platformInitialize` exists only for Windows and PlayStation.
3. **WebSocket is not in WebCore any more.** The single-process `WebSocketChannel` (hybi framing) lives in WebKitLegacy with only a CFNetwork backend; `SocketProvider::createWebSocketChannel` is pure. Dev-mode hot reload needs ZeroG's own channel over `CurlStream`, which connects with a blocking `curl_easy_perform` on one shared thread.
4. **GPU: one upstream path, Skia Ganesh over GLES via EGL** (`SK_ASSUME_GL_ES`). Linux: EGL/GLES. Windows: ANGLE EGL. Vulkan and native Metal are new work; macOS v1 is realistically Skia GL over ANGLE's Metal backend. On `PLATFORM(WIN)` the shared display is a hard-coded ANGLE display; `PLATFORM(ZEROG)` avoids that but loses Win-only branches. The WebKit2 Win compositor (WC*) is not reusable; `GraphicsLayerTextureMapper` is the in-process compositor for v2.
5. **The phase 1 dependency set is larger than the doc's step 2**: ICU, libxml2, SQLite, zlib, HarfBuzz *with* ICU (`hb-icu`), FreeType, libpng, libjpeg-turbo, libwebp (+demux, mux, sharpyuv; excluding it breaks `toDataURL`), **BoringSSL already for PAL's digests**, libpsl (or stubs). Skia's CMake hard-requires Fontconfig off Windows: patch needed. Skia's `third_party/` holds no sources; every dependency comes from the sysroot.
6. **Process-global setup** WebKitLegacy performs before the first `Page`: platform strategies, DOM wrapper hooks, storage tracker, log channels; `BlobRegistry` through `PlatformStrategies`. Four `PageConfiguration` members typed optional are dereferenced unconditionally.
7. **Flags**: `ENABLE_SERVICE_WORKER` no longer exists (always compiled; off at runtime by default). `InspectorClient` is now `InspectorBackendClient`.
8. **Threads and env vars**: every Skia GL context spawns 2 unnamed worker threads; MSAA and other knobs read environment variables (`WEBKIT_SKIA_MSAA_SAMPLE_COUNT`), against the doc's "config, not env vars".
9. **GC signal on Linux**: JSC still checks for an existing handler but now only logs and replaces it; if the host re-installs its handler or blocks the signal, `Thread::suspend` waits forever and the GC deadlocks. Bun's sigaltstack fix is real: upstream's retry tests the handler's own stack pointer. A configurable free signal and the alternate-stack fix are both needed.
10. **Virtual memory reservations exist whatever the allocator**: JSC always reserves a 4 GB Structure heap on 64-bit, the JIT a 1 GB executable pool (x86_64), mimalloc 1 GiB arenas; under `vm.overcommit_memory=2` they count against the commit limit. System malloc only removes Gigacage.

### Decisions for the project owner

- **Licensing: WTF is LGPL-encumbered too**, so the relink obligation covers the whole WTF + JSC + WebCore archive, and files derived from WebKitLegacy still link LGPL WTF. The license table in ZeroG.md needs fixing; the relink kit design is unaffected.
- **Allocator**: system malloc on Linux plus mimalloc on Windows (today), or mimalloc everywhere (identical behaviour on all OSes, optional `MI_SECURE` hardening and a smaller arena reservation)?
- **Windows CRT for host applications**: the bundle is `/MT`; MSVC hosts built `/MD` cannot link it (`/FAILIFMISMATCH`). Ship `/MT` only (single-exe goal), or also an `/MD` bundle?
- **Bindings: sync or async by default.** ZeroG.md says sync, but the Zig example uses `await App.listFiles`. Proposal: an explicit async marker (`zg.Async(T)`) that emits `Promise<T>` in `bindings.d.ts`.
- **External content must be a runtime-level switch** fixed at `zg_runtime_create` (it forces the JIT off for the process), not a context or view option. `include/zerog.h` currently has it in `zg_context_config`.
- **Profiles**: split `dev` (release engine + inspector) from `debug` (assertions), and decide how the inspector frontend is shown (second window, or an external browser over the remote inspector socket).
- **rAF clock per mode**: engine vsync in window mode, explicit timestamp from the app in texture/CPU mode (`RunLoopObserver` does nothing on `RunLoopGeneric`).
- **Dev origins**: an `allowed_origins` list in the context config so the dev server can get bindings and navigation in dev builds only.

| Start from the JSCOnly port, which depends only on ICU, to verify the static WTF+JSC build | CONFIRMED | OptionsJSCOnly.cmake:1 `find_package(Threads REQUIRED)`, :113 `find_package(ICU 70.1 REQUIRED COMPONENTS data i18n uc)`. GLib only when EVENT_LOOP_TYPE=GLib or ENABLE_JSC_GLIB_API (:89-107); libbacktrace optional (:118-123). It forces ENABLE_WEBCORE, WEBKIT, WEBKIT_LEGACY, WEBINSPECTORUI, WEBGL and WEBGPU OFF (:39-44). JSCOnly supports Windows: WIN32 branches in OptionsJSCOnly.cmake:46-87, WTF PlatformJSCOnly.cmake:5-33,74-78 and JSC PlatformJSCOnly.cmake:41-45; webkitdirs.pm:1339 and :2881-2884 (`shouldUseVcpkg { return isWin() \|\| (isJSCOnly() && isAnyWindows()) }`). Source/cmake/FindICU.cmake defers to CMake's built-in FindICU on non-Apple. No WebKit file defines U_STATIC_IMPLEMENTATION. WTF/wtf/CMakeLists.txt:808-810 links ICU::data, ICU::i18n and ICU::uc. | Good first milestone. For static ICU pass -DICU_ROOT=<static prefix> and add -DU_STATIC_IMPLEMENTATION to C/CXX flags; on Windows also point ICU_*_LIBRARY at the sicu*.lib files if FindICU does not find them. ENABLE_API_TESTS is force-set ON on non-Windows JSCOnly (OptionsJSCOnly.cmake:46-50, a plain set() that overrides -D), so gtest gets configured. |
| Step 2: add WebCore with only Skia, HarfBuzz, FreeType, libxml2 and SQLite | REFUTED | WebCore/CMakeLists.txt:2263-2268 always links `set(WebCore_LIBRARIES ICU::uc LibXml2::LibXml2 SQLite3::SQLite3 ZLIB::ZLIB)`. WebCore/platform/Skia.cmake:37-41 links HarfBuzz::HarfBuzz, HarfBuzz::ICU and Skia::Skia, and ComplexTextControllerSkia.cpp:35 includes <hb-icu.h>. Bundled Skia always links JPEG::JPEG and PNG::PNG (skia CMakeLists.txt:972-975) and needs Fontconfig on non-Windows (:3-7). WebCore/platform/ImageDecoders.cmake:37-40 links JPEG::JPEG, PNG::PNG and WebP::demux, and every existing Platform*.cmake includes it. — skeptic: Paths are relative to C:/Users/dzonerzy/CppProjects/ZeroG/third_party/webkit. - Source/WebCore/CMakeLists.txt:2263-2268: set(WebCore_LIBRARIES ICU::uc LibXml2::LibXml2 SQLite3::SQLite3 ZLIB::ZLIB) - Source/WebCore/platform/Skia.cmake:37-41: list(APPEND WebCore_LIBRARIES HarfBuzz::HarfBuzz HarfBuzz::ICU Skia::Skia) - Source/WebCore/platform/graphics/skia/… | The minimum set for step 2 is ICU, libxml2, SQLite, zlib, HarfBuzz built with ICU (hb-icu), FreeType (non-Windows), libpng and libjpeg-turbo, plus Fontconfig unless Skia's CMake is patched, plus libwebp if ImageDecoders.cmake is included. This also contradicts the dependency table's 'HarfBuzz without ICU'. |
| Minimum CMake and C++ standard (implicit in the build plan) | CONFIRMED | CMakeLists.txt:9 `cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.20)`. CMakePresets.json has cmakeMinimumRequired 3.20 and no JSCOnly preset. OptionsCommon.cmake:1-3 sets CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 23, CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON and CMAKE_CXX_EXTENSIONS OFF. OptionsMSVC.cmake:86-92 sets _HAS_EXCEPTIONS=0, /EHs-c-, /GR-. WebKitCompilerFlags.cmake:201-204 sets -fno-exceptions, -fno-rtti and -fcoroutines for non-clang-cl. | Host code that includes WebKit headers must be C++23 with no RTTI. The C API layer (zg_*) should be the only boundary that zig sees. |

**Facts the doc misses**

- New PORT values are rejected unless added to the hardcoded ALL_PORTS list in Source/cmake/WebKitCommon.cmake:76-95, so a patch to an upstream file is unavoidable. A build dir also stamps PORT, CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE, DEVELOPER_MODE and ENABLE_SANITIZERS and fails if they change (WebKitCommon.cmake:12-37, .webkit-config-stamp).
- Platform macros: without a BUILDING_<X>__ define, PlatformLegacy.h:48-77 sets PLATFORM(WIN) on Windows and PLATFORM(MAC)/PLATFORM(COCOA) on macOS, and BPlatform.h:121-129 sets BPLATFORM_WIN. JSCOnly avoids this with -DBUILDING_JSCONLY__ (OptionsJSCOnly.cmake:11), which deliberately provides no PLATFORM() at all. PlayStation uses -DWTF_PLATFORM_PLAYSTATION=1 plus PlatformEnablePlayStation.h (PlatformEnable.h:122-124). ZeroG needs a header patch adding BUILDING_ZEROG__ / WTF_PLATFORM_ZEROG, and probably a PlatformEnableZeroG.h.
- WEBKIT_OPTION_BEGIN() is where every ENABLE_*/USE_* option is defined (WebKitFeatures.cmake:96-398). OptionsZeroG.cmake must call WEBKIT_OPTION_BEGIN/END and set defaults with WEBKIT_OPTION_DEFAULT_PORT_VALUE. PRIVATE options are only mark_as_advanced and can still be set with -D (WebKitFeatures.cmake:524-527). A plain set() after WEBKIT_OPTION_END overrides user -D values; JSCOnly does this for ENABLE_API_TESTS.
- PAL needs its own PlatformZeroG.cmake in Source/WebCore/PAL/pal/, which the doc omits. PlayStation's version adds crypto/openssl/CryptoDigestOpenSSL.cpp, system/ClockGeneric.cpp, system/Sound.cpp and text/KillRing.cpp and links OpenSSL::Crypto, so BoringSSL is needed already at the PAL level.
- Windows: USE_SYSTEM_MALLOC=ON is a hard #error ('System malloc configuration is not supported in Windows since aligned memory cannot be freed via ::free. Use mimalloc instead', bmalloc/bmalloc/BPlatform.h:431-433). libpas is enabled on Windows/Linux x86_64/arm64/Darwin (BPlatform.h:400).
- Windows final link requirements set by OptionsMSVC.cmake, which an external zig/lld-link step must replicate: clang_rt.builtins-x86_64.lib (:128-141, found under <clang-cl>/../lib/clang/<major>/lib/windows, REQUIRED), /OPT:NOICF /OPT:REF /DEBUG (:83, ICF is unsafe for WebKit), /STACK:8388608 (:143), /INCREMENTAL:NO, and AT&T inline asm (/clang:-masm=att).
- The upstream Windows flow (build-webkit/build-jsc) uses vcpkg manifest mode: root vcpkg.json depends on icu, vcpkg-configuration.json pins microsoft/vcpkg plus the WebKitForWindows/WebKitRequirements registry for icu, curl, openssl, zlib and others, and the overlay triplet WebKitLibraries/triplets/x64-windows-webkit.cmake is dynamic CRT, dynamic libs, release only. The 'web' feature uses libressl (curl[libressl]) rather than BoringSSL (vcpkg.json).
- On clang (non-MSVC) with precompiled headers enabled, the JIT/DFG/FTL/bytecode/llint objects (JavaScriptCoreJIT) and the WebCore JSBindings/Style/DOMAndRendering/Inspector objects are not stored in libJavaScriptCore.a or libWebCore.a. They are only INTERFACE link items (WebKitMacros.cmake:409-437). Linux thin archives are on by default (OptionsCommon.cmake:167-182). Both break 'collect the archives and link with zig'; use -DUSE_THIN_ARCHIVES=OFF -DCMAKE_DISABLE_PRECOMPILE_HEADERS=ON or harvest the object lists.
- The doc says zig does the final link. Zig links its bundled LLVM libc++ by default, while clang on Linux compiles WebKit against the system libstdc++, and clang-cl compiles against the MSVC STL. Mixing them breaks the C++ ABI. Either compile WebKit with the same stdlib/toolchain as the final link (e.g. CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER='zig c++') or have zig only link C objects and let a clang/lld step carry the C++ runtime. This is inferred from toolchain behaviour and needs a test build.
- Bundled Skia (Source/ThirdParty/skia/CMakeLists.txt) has a Windows branch using the DirectWrite font manager (:814-826), and its non-Windows branch is FreeType+Fontconfig (:827-854) with no CoreText/macOS branch. The only GPU backend is Ganesh GL via EGL or epoxy (:856-867), with no Vulkan, Metal or D3D sources. Skia is add_library(Skia STATIC) (:21).
- WebCore's Skia path requires HarfBuzz built with ICU: Skia.cmake:37-41 links HarfBuzz::ICU and ComplexTextControllerSkia.cpp:35 includes <hb-icu.h>. This contradicts the doc's 'HarfBuzz without ICU'.
- There is a MinGW path in WebKitCompilerFlags.cmake:211-215 (-mno-ms-bitfields, __USE_MINGW_ANSI_STDIO) for WIN32 without clang-cl, and OptionsJSCOnly only includes OptionsMSVC when MSVC is set. It is not exercised by build scripts or CI, so clang-cl remains the only supported Windows toolchain.
- JSCOnly sets Windows defines -DNTDDI_VERSION=0x0A000006 -D_WIN32_WINNT=0x0A00, so the minimum target is Windows 10 1809. It also sets -DNOCRYPT -D_WINSOCKAPI_= -DUNICODE (OptionsJSCOnly.cmake:59-74).
- Source/WTF/wtf, WebCore and PAL contain orphan PlatformHaiku.cmake files and PlatformLegacy.h has a BUILDING_HAIKU__ branch, but there is no OptionsHaiku.cmake and Haiku is not in ALL_PORTS. It is a small reference for adding a new PLATFORM() macro.
- Local machine state, checked during this audit: cmake is not on PATH in Git Bash (it exists inside the VS 2022 install, Common7/IDE/CommonExtensions/Microsoft/CMake). ruby and gperf are missing. perl resolves to MSYS /usr/bin/perl, a POSIX-path perl that may mishandle Windows paths, so prefer Strawberry Perl. clang-cl 19.1.7 is installed at C:/Program Files/LLVM with lib/clang/19/lib/windows/clang_rt.builtins-x86_64.lib present. ninja resolves to C:/Windows/system32/ninja. vcpkg is at C:/vcpkg.

<details><summary>Concrete starting material</summary>

```text
=== Linux x64, clang, JSCOnly, static WTF+bmalloc+JSC ===
# Prereqs: cmake>=3.20, ninja, clang>=18 (19+ preferred), perl>=5.10 with JSON::PP, python3, ruby>=2.5, ICU>=70.1 (static: build ICU with --enable-static --disable-shared)
cmake -S third_party/webkit -B build/jsc-linux -G Ninja \
  -DPORT=JSCOnly \
  -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release \
  -DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=clang -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=clang++ \
  -DENABLE_STATIC_JSC=ON \
  -DUSE_THIN_ARCHIVES=OFF \
  -DCMAKE_DISABLE_PRECOMPILE_HEADERS=ON \
  -DENABLE_TOOLS=OFF \
  -DICU_ROOT=/path/to/icu-static \
  -DCMAKE_C_FLAGS=-DU_STATIC_IMPLEMENTATION -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS=-DU_STATIC_IMPLEMENTATION
cmake --build build/jsc-linux --target jsc
# Outputs: build/jsc-linux/lib/{libbmalloc.a,libWTF.a,libJavaScriptCore.a}, bin/jsc
# Notes: USE_THIN_ARCHIVES=OFF makes the .a self-contained. PCH off keeps the JavaScriptCoreJIT objects inside libJavaScriptCore.a (WebKitMacros.cmake:409-437).
# Optional: -DDEVELOPER_MODE=ON (what build-jsc passes), -DENABLE_JIT=OFF -DENABLE_C_LOOP=ON for the interpreter-only build.
# Upstream script equivalent: perl Tools/Scripts/build-jsc --jsc-only --release --cmakeargs="-DENABLE_STATIC_JSC=ON -DUSE_THIN_ARCHIVES=OFF"

=== Windows x64, clang-cl + Ninja, JSCOnly, static WTF+bmalloc+JSC ===
# Run from an "x64 Native Tools Command Prompt for VS 2022" so the Windows SDK/LIB/INCLUDE env is set.
# Prereqs: CMake>=3.20 (the VS-bundled one is fine), Ninja, LLVM clang-cl (19.1.7 present locally), Strawberry Perl, Python 3, Ruby>=2.5 (RubyInstaller); gperf is only needed once WebCore is on.
# (a) Upstream-faithful (dynamic ICU via vcpkg, /MD):
cmake -S third_party\webkit -B build\jsc-win -G Ninja ^
  -DPORT=JSCOnly -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release ^
  -DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=clang-cl -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=clang-cl ^
  -DCMAKE_LINKER=lld-link ^
  -DENABLE_STATIC_JSC=ON ^
  -DCMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE=%VCPKG_ROOT%\scripts\buildsystems\vcpkg.cmake ^
  -DVCPKG_TARGET_TRIPLET=x64-windows-webkit
cmake --build build\jsc-win --target jsc
# (b) ZeroG-style (static ICU, no vcpkg): replace the two VCPKG lines with
#   -DICU_ROOT=C:\deps\icu-static -DCMAKE_C_FLAGS=-DU_STATIC_IMPLEMENTATION -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS=-DU_STATIC_IMPLEMENTATION
#   (ICU built with clang-cl and the same CRT; set ICU_UC_LIBRARY/ICU_I18N_LIBRARY/ICU_DATA_LIBRARY to sicuuc/sicuin/sicudt .lib if FindICU misses them)
# /MT is NOT reachable with -D: OptionsMSVC.cmake:58 hard-sets MultiThreadedDLL. Override it in OptionsZeroG.cmake after include(OptionsMSVC):
#   set(CMAKE_MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY MultiThreaded)
# Upstream script equivalent: set VCPKG_ROOT, then perl Tools\Scripts\build-jsc --jsc-only --release --cmakeargs="-DENABLE_STATIC_JSC=ON"
# Outputs: build\jsc-win\lib\{bmalloc.lib,WTF.lib,JavaScriptCore.lib}, bin\jsc.exe. Final link needs DbgHelp shlwapi synchronization winmm ws2_32 clang_rt.builtins-x86_64.lib /OPT:NOICF /STACK:8388608.

Threading.cpp:511-513 registers the window class in WTF::initialize(). | Don't use RunLoopWin for game or own-loop mode, because cycle() would steal and dispatch the host's Win32 messages. It must not call back into RunLoop (that deadlocks on m_loopLock) and must be thread-safe. A small WTF patch is still needed to expose the next timer deadline (e.g. RunLoop::secondsUntilNextTimer()), because the callback fires when a timer is armed, not when it expires. Without that, the host can't compute its idle-wait timeout. |

If the host later re-installs its own handler, or blocks the signal with pthread_sigmask, Thread::suspend waits forever on globalSemaphoreForSuspendResume (:592) and the GC deadlocks. Choosing a free signal is still required. |

It returns false if isThreadSuspendResumeSignalConfigured is already set, and otherwise sets g_wtfConfig.sigThreadSuspendResume and isUserSpecifiedThreadSuspendResumeSignalConfigured. It is declared in the private header JSBasePrivate.h:50-59 ('Call this function before any of JSC initialization starts'). | zg_runtime_create must call JSConfigureSignalForGC(cfg.gc_signal) before anything that triggers WTF::initialize(), and check the return value. A user-specified value also turns off the JSC_SIGNAL_FOR_GC env override. For a real-time default, compute SIGRTMIN+n at runtime: glibc's SIGRTMIN is not a compile-time constant. |

ThreadingPOSIX.cpp:150-160: if the handler's stack pointer is not inside thread->m_stack (alt stack), it sets m_platformRegisters = nullptr, posts the semaphore and returns. Thread::suspend (:586-598) retries with Thread::yield() until it succeeds. Upstream code never uses sigaltstack or SA_ONSTACK (suspend handler flags SA_RESTART\|SA_SIGINFO at :221; trap handlers SA_SIGINFO only at Signals.cpp:590). I could not check the Bun PR (external). — skeptic: C:/Users/dzonerzy/CppProjects/ZeroG/third_party/webkit/Source/WTF/wtf/posix/ThreadingPOSIX.cpp:150-161: `void* approximateStackPointer = currentStackPointer(); if (!thread->m_stack.contains(approximateStackPointer)) { thread->m_platformRegisters = nullptr; globalSemaphoreForSuspendResume->post(); return; }`. This is the handler's own stack pointer, not the one saved in the ucontext. Threadi… | There is no hang, but there can be a busy retry loop while a thread runs a host handler on its alt stack. JSC's SIGSEGV handler has no SA_ONSTACK, so a stack-overflow SIGSEGV that reaches JSC first cannot run. CI should run with a crash handler plus sigaltstack, as the doc says. |
| JSC handles CPU trap signals (SIGSEGV, SIGBUS, SIGILL), e.g. for WebAssembly memory, and the handlers must chain to the host's | PARTIAL | Signals.cpp:410-424 maps AccessFault->SIGSEGV+SIGBUS, IllegalInstruction->SIGILL, Usr->SIGUSR2, FloatingPoint->SIGFPE, Breakpoint->SIGTRAP, Abort->SIGABRT. In the library only AccessFault is registered: VMTraps.cpp:208-216 (signal-based VM traps, when !Options::usePollingTraps(), default false at OptionsList.h:551) and WasmFaultSignalHandler.cpp:143-162 (Options::useWasmFaultSignalHandler, default true at OptionsList.h:660). SIGILL/SIGFPE/SIGTRAP/SIGABRT/SIGUSR2 are registered only by the jsc shell (jsc.cpp:3762,4292-4305). Handlers are installed in SignalHandlers::finalize (Signals.cpp:564-597) from Config::finalize at first VM creation (VM.cpp:568), and the old sigaction is saved in handlers.oldActions. On POSIX, jscSignalHandler (:476-544) chains: if no JSC handler returns Handled it calls oldAction.sa_sigaction, otherwise it restores SIG_DFL and returns. On Darwin, Mach exception po… | SIGILL is not involved. Chaining already exists, but it is one-way: JSC chains to whatever was installed before the first VM, and a host crash handler installed after that must chain back to JSC itself. With ENABLE_WEBASSEMBLY off plus usePollingTraps=true, JSC installs no SIGSEGV handler at all, which is a good option for the minimal profile. |
| macOS uses Mach ports and Windows SuspendThread, so the problem only affects Linux | CONFIRMED | ThreadingPOSIX.cpp:575-583 Darwin Thread::suspend = thread_suspend() (Mach thread API, no signal). ThreadingWin.cpp:239/248 SuspendThread/ResumeThread. PlatformHave.h:227-228 HAVE_MACH_EXCEPTIONS for OS(DARWIN) (not only PLATFORM(COCOA)). OptionsList.h:554 useMachForExceptions default true. JSBasePrivate.h:50 JSConfigureSignalForGC is not available on Apple or Windows. | Correct for the GC signal. On macOS, JSC sets thread-level Mach exception ports (Signals.cpp:386 thread_set_exception_ports) on threads that run JSC, and it starts a Mach exception handler thread. Crashpad uses task-level ports, so it still sees faults that JSC doesn't handle. |
| Every engine thread has a recognizable name that shows up in profilers and crash dumps | PARTIAL | Thread::create(ASCIILiteral threadName, ...) (Threading.h:136). Names include "JSC Heap Collector Thread" (Collector.cpp:77), "HeapHelper" on Linux / "Heap Helper Thread" elsewhere (HeapHelperPool.cpp:42-46), "JIT Worklist Helper Thread" (JITWorklistThread.cpp:93), "JSC VMTraps Signal Sender" (VMTraps.cpp:358), "org.webkit.ImageDecoder" (ImageFrameWorkQueue.cpp:64), "WebCore: Worker" (DedicatedWorkerThread.h:63). On Linux, normalizeThreadName (Threading.cpp:179-203) keeps the text after the last '.' and then keeps the RIGHTMOST 15 chars: 'JSC Heap Collector Thread' becomes 'ollector Thread'. On Windows, initializeCurrentThreadInternal (ThreadingWin.cpp:141-154) only raises the old MS_VC_EXCEPTION 0x406D1388, which only an attached debugger sees. There is no SetThreadDescription anywhere in WTF. MainThreadWin.cpp:44 also names the host's thread 'Main Thread'. setCurrentThreadIsUserIntera… | To get names in Windows minidumps, ETW and Process Explorer, patch ThreadingWin to call SetThreadDescription. On Linux, choose engine thread names that are at most 15 chars, or change the truncation. |
| GC, JIT and decode thread counts are configurable | PARTIAL | JSC::Options (OptionsList.h): numberOfGCMarkers (:417, computeNumberOfGCMarkers(8); HeapHelperPool.cpp:48 ensureThreads(numberOfGCMarkers-1)), useConcurrentGC (:209), useConcurrentJIT (:291), numberOfBaselineCompilerThreads/numberOfDFGCompilerThreads/numberOfFTLCompilerThreads/numberOfWasmCompilerThreads (:294-297), priorityDelta* (:302-304), useParallelMarkingConstraintSolver (:418). They can be set from code through JSC::initialize(lambda) / Options::initialize(callback); the callback runs last (Options.cpp:1141) and options are finalized after that. Options::setOption/setOptions are JS_EXPORT_PRIVATE (Options.h:126-130). Image decoding is NOT a pool: ImageFrameWorkQueue::start (ImageFrameWorkQueue.cpp:64) calls WorkQueue::create per BitmapImageSource, and WorkQueueGeneric.cpp:44 creates a new RunLoop thread (RunLoop::create) for each queue. — skeptic: All paths are relative to C:/Use… | Set the GC and JIT counts in the JSC::initialize customization lambda before the first VM. Note that env vars JSC_<option> (Options.cpp:1105, Linux/Cocoa) and the heuristic getenv path (non-Cocoa) still apply, so the host's environment can change engine behaviour. Clear them or override them in the callback. A bounded decode pool needs a WebCore patch, e.g. a shared WorkQueue/ConcurrentWorkQueue in ImageFrameWorkQueue. |
| Web Workers run on engine threads, each with its own run loop, and share heap and allocator with the host | PARTIAL | WorkerThread.cpp:149 Thread::create(threadName(), ..., ThreadType::JavaScript). WebCore/workers/WorkerRunLoop.h:47-126 is WebCore's own MessageQueue-based WorkerRunLoop, not WTF::RunLoop. WorkerGlobalScope.cpp:117: a worker gets its OWN JSC::VM::create(JSC::HeapType::Medium), so the JS heap is not shared with the main VM. Only the malloc allocator is shared. No WebKit2 dependency: WorkerMessagingProxy is in WebCore (WorkerMessagingProxy.cpp:184-189). ChromeClient::createWorkerClient defaults to nullptr (ChromeClient.cpp:67-70). — skeptic: All paths are relative to C:/Users/dzonerzy/CppProjects/ZeroG/third_party/webkit/Source. - WebCore/workers/WorkerMessagingProxy.cpp:168: WorkerParameters{..., WorkerThreadMode::CreateNewThread, ...}. Line 180: DedicatedWorkerThread::create(...). Lines 316-318: postTaskToLoader -> ScriptExecutionContext::postTaskTo(m_loaderContextIdentifier) (main threa… | Workers need nothing from WebKit2. Worker loads and messages go through WorkerMessagingProxy to the main thread, so workers stall if the host stops pumping zg_runtime_update. Also correct the doc's 'una sola VM': each worker has a separate VM/heap, and memory limits must account for that. |
| The whole API runs on one thread, and calls from another thread fail explicitly in debug | CONFIRMED | WTF::isMainThread() (MainThreadGeneric.cpp:52-55, MainThreadWin.cpp:47-50) and RunLoop::isMain()/isCurrent() (RunLoop.h:107-108, RunLoop.cpp:122-126) are available. RunLoop::dispatch is thread-safe (RunLoop.cpp:160-173), and callOnMainThread exists (MainThread.cpp:81-91). | Build zg_promise_resolve and other off-thread entry points on callOnMainThread / RunLoop::mainSingleton().dispatch. The wakeup callback then signals the host. |

**Facts the doc misses**

- A new PORT must define its own BUILDING_<PORT>__ and a branch in WTF/wtf/PlatformLegacy.h:48-76. Without one, a macOS build becomes PLATFORM(MAC)/COCOA (USE_CF, USE_FOUNDATION, cocoa RunLoop, CF shared timer) and a Windows build becomes PLATFORM(WIN). JSCOnly avoids this with -DBUILDING_JSCONLY__ (OptionsJSCOnly.cmake:11; PlatformLegacy.h:55 'JSCOnly does not provide PLATFORM() macro'). PlayStation instead adds -DWTF_PLATFORM_PLAYSTATION=1 (OptionsPlayStation.cmake:10).
- WebCore's main-thread shared timer (it drives all DOM timers) is chosen by OS, not by event loop. MainThreadSharedTimer.h:33,41,71 and .cpp:49 use '#if !USE(CF) && !OS(WINDOWS)' to choose RunLoop::Timer, so on Windows WebCore always uses platform/win/MainThreadSharedTimerWin.cpp. That file creates an HWND_MESSAGE window (:86-87), uses SetTimer/PostMessage (:95) and timeBeginPeriod for timers under 16 ms. With RunLoopGeneric on Windows, DOM timers only fire if the host pumps Win32 messages on the main thread. Patch the condition to !USE(WINDOWS_EVENT_LOOP), or don't add MainThreadSharedTimerWin.cpp in the ZeroG WebCore platform cmake.
- WebCore::RunLoopObserver is a stub on non-CF/non-GLib loops (RunLoopObserver.cpp:52-67: schedule/invalidate do nothing, isScheduled returns false). OpportunisticTaskScheduler uses it, and so do the commonVM GC activity callbacks (CommonVM.cpp:70-73 set FullGC/EdenGC callbacks; OpportunisticTaskScheduler.cpp:186-221 fall back to m_runLoopObserver->schedule() when the page is busy). On Generic those deferred GCs never run from the timer path. Implement RunLoopObserver for the generic loop (before/after-cycle hooks in RunLoop::cycle) or have the embedding layer handle it. Rendering updates also need the embedder to schedule them (ChromeClient), because nothing in Generic observes loop activity.
- The generic wakeup callback runs while RunLoop::m_loopLock is held (RunLoopGeneric.cpp:262-269), on arbitrary threads: network, JIT, GC timers (JSRunLoopTimer), workers. It must only signal an OS primitive. Calling RunLoop::wakeUp/dispatch/timer APIs from inside it deadlocks. setWakeUpCallback is static and applies to RunLoop::currentSingleton(), so call it on the main thread after initializeMainThread.
- The GC suspend signal is configured and installed inside WTF::initialize() (Threading.cpp:507 -> ThreadingPOSIX.cpp:195-238). WTF::initialize() runs lazily from the first Thread::currentSingleton() (ThreadingPOSIX.cpp:550-553). So JSConfigureSignalForGC must be the very first WebKit call in the process. If the host blocks that signal in a thread that has entered JSC (pthread_sigmask), GC/VMTraps/SamplingProfiler suspension deadlocks in sem_wait (ThreadingPOSIX.cpp:592). initializeCurrentThreadEvenIfNonWTFCreated (:248-256) unblocks it only once, at thread registration.
- POSIX trap chaining calls oldAction.sa_sigaction whenever it is non-null (Signals.cpp:530-541). If the host had set SIG_IGN (value 1) for SIGSEGV/SIGBUS before the first VM, JSC jumps to address 1. Old handlers installed without SA_SIGINFO get called with 3 args (usually harmless). JSC installs with sa_mask = all signals except the GC signal and without SA_ONSTACK (:584-590).
- Trap handlers and the config freeze happen at FIRST VM creation (VM.cpp:568 Config::finalize), not at JSC::initialize. A host crash handler (Crashpad/Breakpad/Sentry) installed after the first zg_view/VM replaces JSC's SIGSEGV handler unless it chains. VMTraps and Wasm OOB then turn into crashes. Document the init order, or reinstall/verify after the host's crash-reporter init.
- Options::usePollingTraps (OptionsList.h:551, default false) controls whether VMTraps uses SIGSEGV-based halt instructions plus a 'JSC VMTraps Signal Sender' WorkQueue thread that suspends the mutator (VMTraps.cpp:208-216, 309, 358). InitializeThreading.cpp:144-148 installs VMTraps signals only when !usePollingTraps. Setting usePollingTraps=true removes one SIGSEGV user and one suspend user; Wasm disabled removes the other.
- Env vars prefixed JSC_ are applied to any JSC option at init (Options.cpp:1105-1112 on Linux/Cocoa, plus overrideOptionWithHeuristic getenv for all options on non-Cocoa, Options.cpp:395-420, 1118-1121). A host environment can therefore change the engine (JIT, GC threads, etc.). zg_runtime_config values should be applied in the Options customization callback, which gets the last word (Options.cpp:1141).
- On Windows the config pages are NOT frozen (WTFConfig.cpp:228-233 FIXME, no VirtualProtect), so that hardening is missing there. WebConfig::g_config is an extern "C" global named 'g_config' (WTFConfig.cpp:89, WTFConfig.h:51, also GigacageConfig.h:39). It collides with any other JSC/WebKit in the host process unless symbols are localized.
- RunLoopWin (if it were used) creates a message-only window per thread RunLoop, including every WorkQueue thread (RunLoopWin.cpp:161-166). Its cycle() dispatches every thread message through TranslateMessage/DispatchMessage. Avoid it for embedding.
- RunLoopGeneric's RunLoop::cycle() ignores RunLoopMode and always returns CycleResult::Continue. Each call fires timers and drains m_nextIteration exactly once (RunLoopGeneric.cpp:277-281, RunLoop.cpp:128-158). Work dispatched during the cycle runs on the NEXT cycle, so zg_runtime_update may need to loop until idle or a time budget runs out.
- Image decoding creates a new WorkQueue and therefore a new OS thread per async-decoded image source (ImageFrameWorkQueue.cpp:64; WorkQueueGeneric.cpp:44 RunLoop::create -> Thread::create, RunLoop.cpp:108-120). That is unbounded and conflicts with the doc's 'pool di worker' and configurable decode thread count.
- Each Web Worker gets a separate JSC::VM (WorkerGlobalScope.cpp:117, HeapType::Medium). That VM has its own GC threads and timers, and its own worker thread is added to the threads that GC/VMTraps suspend with the GC signal.
- Linux thread-name truncation keeps the RIGHTMOST 15 characters (Threading.cpp:196-199), so long engine names become unreadable. Windows naming only works with a debugger attached (ThreadingWin.cpp:129-154, MS_VC_EXCEPTION). WTF also names the host's thread 'Main Thread' on Windows (MainThreadWin.cpp:44).
- On macOS with a custom (non-Cocoa) port, OS(DARWIN) code still turns on: HAVE_MACH_EXCEPTIONS (PlatformHave.h:227), a Mach exception handler thread, thread_suspend, and pthread_main_np. RunLoopGeneric does not drain the libdispatch main queue (drainDispatchMainQueue exists only for GLib/Windows, RunLoop.h:315-319). In window mode on macOS the NSApplication/CFRunLoop must stay in charge and call RunLoop::cycle() from a CFRunLoopSource that the wakeup callback signals.

<details><summary>Concrete starting material</summary>

```text
CMake (Options<ZeroG>.cmake), modeled on OptionsJSCOnly.cmake:11,102-111:
  add_definitions(-DBUILDING_ZEROG__)        # plus a branch in WTF/wtf/PlatformLegacy.h (or define nothing, like JSCOnly, but then PLATFORM(MAC/WIN) is blocked only if the elif chain is handled)
  SET_AND_EXPOSE_TO_BUILD(USE_GENERIC_EVENT_LOOP 1)
  SET_AND_EXPOSE_TO_BUILD(WTF_DEFAULT_EVENT_LOOP 0)
PlatformZeroG.cmake for WTF: generic/RunLoopGeneric.cpp, generic/WorkQueueGeneric.cpp; on WIN32 win/MainThreadWin.cpp, win/ThreadingWin.cpp, win/SignalsWin.cpp (copy the PlatformJSCOnly.cmake:5-35 list); on POSIX generic/MainThreadGeneric.cpp, posix/ThreadingPOSIX.cpp (PlatformJSCOnly.cmake:36-60); APPLE adds the mig MachExceptions step (PlatformJSCOnly.cmake:78-104).
WebCore: do NOT compile platform/win/MainThreadSharedTimerWin.cpp. Patch MainThreadSharedTimer.h/.cpp: '!USE(CF) && !OS(WINDOWS)' -> '!USE(CF) && !USE(WINDOWS_EVENT_LOOP)'.

Init sequence for zg_runtime_create (on the creating thread, before anything else touches WebKit):
  #include <JavaScriptCore/JSBasePrivate.h>
  #if !defined(__APPLE__) && !defined(_WIN32)
  if (!JSConfigureSignalForGC(cfg->gc_signal ? cfg->gc_signal : SIGRTMIN + k)) fail("WebKit already initialized");
  #endif
  JSC::initialize([&] {   // Options customization; runs after JSC_* env parsing, gets the last word
      JSC::Options::numberOfGCMarkers() = cfg->gc_threads;
      JSC::Options::numberOfDFGCompilerThreads() = ...; JSC::Options::numberOfFTLCompilerThreads() = ...;
      JSC::Options::numberOfBaselineCompilerThreads() = ...;
      JSC::Options::useJIT() = jit_enabled;
      JSC::Options::usePollingTraps() = true;          // optional: no SIGSEGV-based VM traps
      JSC::Options::useWasmFaultSignalHandler() = false; // if Wasm is kept but signals are not wanted
  });
  WTF::initializeMainThread();       // RELEASE_ASSERTs uid==1: must be first WTF thread
  WebCore::populateJITOperations();  // same as ScriptController::initializeMainThread()
  WTF::RunLoop::setWakeUpCallback([fd]{ uint64_t one=1; write(fd,&one,8); /* or SetEvent(h) */ });  // runs under m_loopLock, any thread

zg_runtime_update: WTF::RunLoop::cycle(); (maybe loop while work was produced / within budget)
zg_runtime_run: RunLoop::run() (Generic Drain blocks on WTF::Condition) or a native loop (Win32 MsgWaitForMultipleObjects / CFRunLoop) waiting on the wakeup handle and the next-timer timeout, then cycle().

Minimal WTF patch for idle-wait timeouts (RunLoopGeneric):
  WTF_EXPORT_PRIVATE std::optional<MonotonicTime> RunLoop::nextTimerFireTime() { Locker l{m_loopLock}; if (m_schedules.isEmpty()) return std::nullopt; return m_schedules.first()->scheduledTimePoint(); }

Key files: Source/WTF/wtf/RunLoop.h, RunLoop.cpp, generic/RunLoopGeneric.cpp, win/RunLoopWin.cpp, MainThread.cpp, generic/MainThreadGeneric.cpp, win/MainThreadWin.cpp, Threading.cpp, posix/ThreadingPOSIX.cpp (GC signal :195-238, suspend :575-625), win/ThreadingWin.cpp, threads/Signals.cpp (:476-597), win/SignalsWin.cpp, WTFConfig.cpp; JavaScriptCore/API/JSBase.cpp:185-193, JSBasePrivate.h:50-59, runtime/InitializeThreading.cpp, runtime/Options.cpp, runtime/OptionsList.h, runtime/VMTraps.cpp, wasm/WasmFaultSignalHandler.cpp; WebCore/platform/MainThreadSharedTimer.{h,cpp}, platform/win/MainThreadSharedTimerWin.cpp, platform/RunLoopObserver.cpp, page/OpportunisticTaskScheduler.cpp, bindings/js/CommonVM.cpp, bindings/js/ScriptController.cpp:120-127, platform/WebCoreMainThread.cpp, workers/WorkerThread.cpp, workers/WorkerGlobalScope.cpp:117, workers/WorkerRunLoop.h, platform/graphics/ImageFrameWorkQueue.cpp:64.

Suggested CI checks: (1) host installs SIGUSR1 handler + sigaltstack crash handler before zg_runtime_create -> GC must not hang; (2) host crash handler installed after first view must chain; (3) zg_* call from a second thread before create -> clean error rather than the uid==1 RELEASE_ASSERT; (4) Windows DOM setTimeout fires with no Win32 message pump once the shared-timer patch is in.
```

</details>

## WebCore embedding layer without WebKit2 (Port del motore, Livelli/Layer di embedding, Riferimenti da studiare, Binding JavaScript dirette/Installazione, Storage). Verified against the WebKit checkout at 31a83a39d34abbefba1e6ba91b1ff0dd5f8946fc.

| Claim | Final | Evidence | Implication |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The engine can be a port of its own built directly on WTF + JavaScriptCore + WebCore, with no WebKit2 layer. | CONFIRMED | WebCore/page/Page.h:411 `WEBCORE_EXPORT static Ref<Page> create(PageConfiguration&&);` LocalFrame.h:145 `void init();` and :151 `void createView(const IntSize&, ...)`. WebCore/loader/EmptyClients.cpp:1291 `pageConfigurationWithEmptyClients(...)` builds a complete in-process Page with no WebKit2. Source/CMakeLists.txt:83-89 gates WebKitLegacy and WebKit as separate subdirectories, so a PORT can set ENABLE_WEBKIT=OFF and ENABLE_WEBKIT_LEGACY=OFF and still build WebCore. | Feasible. Set ENABLE_WEBKIT OFF and ENABLE_WEBKIT_LEGACY OFF in OptionsZeroG.cmake. The embedding layer builds Page::create(PageConfiguration&&), then calls localMainFrame->init() and localMainFrame->createView(...) itself. |
| Upstream has removed the single-process path for non-Apple ports (WinCairo WebKit1 deleted). | CONFIRMED | cmake/OptionsWin.cmake:31 `set(ENABLE_WEBKIT_LEGACY OFF)`; OptionsPlayStation.cmake:27 and OptionsJSCOnly.cmake:40 are also OFF; only OptionsCocoa.cmake:223 has it ON. Source/WebKitLegacy has only cf/, ios/ and mac/ subdirectories and only PlatformCocoa.cmake, with no PlatformWin.cmake or win/ directory. The mailing-list URL itself cannot be checked offline. | No upstream non-Apple single-process embedding exists to copy. Everything in the embedding layer is new code, ported from WebKitLegacy (mac) or from WebKit/WebProcess/WebCoreSupport. |
| The old curl implementation of ResourceHandle is gone. | CONFIRMED | There is no ResourceHandleCurl.cpp in WebCore/platform/network/curl, and platform/Curl.cmake has no ResourceHandle entry. ResourceHandle.h/.cpp still exist and are compiled (Sources.txt:2972), but ResourceHandle.cpp:312-345 under `#if USE(SOUP) \|\| USE(CURL)` makes start(), cancel(), platformSetDefersLoading() and platformLoadResourceSynchronously() all `ASSERT_NOT_REACHED()`. The only real implementation is cocoa/ResourceHandleCocoa.mm. One exception: BlobRegistryImpl.cpp:75-82 registers a built-in ResourceHandle constructor for `blob:` (BlobResourceHandle), so ResourceHandle::create() still works for blob URLs (ResourceHandle.cpp:96-101). | ResourceLoader::start() (ResourceLoader.cpp:285 calls ResourceHandle::create) cannot be used for http, https or app://. It works only for data: (loadDataURL at :257) and blob:. ZeroG's LoaderStrategy must drive the loaders itself, the way WebKit2 does. |

InProcessIDBServer uses WorkQueue (generic), which posts back with callOnMainThread, so zg_runtime_update() must drain the main RunLoop. |

The request logic is in WebKit2: WebKit/NetworkProcess/curl/NetworkDataTaskCurl.cpp handles redirects, auth and cookies (appendCookieHeader :541, handleCookieHeaders :551). NetworkStorageSession has moved out of WebCore into WebKit/Shared/NetworkStorageSession.h (324 lines), with its curl part in WebKit/NetworkProcess/curl/NetworkStorageSessionCurl.cpp (264 lines). CurlRequest.cpp contains no cookie handling. — skeptic: All paths are under C:/Users/dzonerzy/CppProjects/ZeroG/third_party/webkit/Source. Curl.cmake links `CURL::libcurl LibPSL::LibPSL OpenSSL::SSL`, so BoringSSL must provide an OpenSSL::SSL target and libpsl is a hard dependency. |

| The PlayStation and JSCOnly ports are CMake builds with no GLib, RunLoopGeneric, curl networking and Skia planned. | PARTIAL | JSCOnly does not build WebCore at all (OptionsJSCOnly.cmake:39 `set(ENABLE_WEBCORE OFF)`, :41 ENABLE_WEBKIT OFF), so it is only a reference for WTF and JSC. PlayStation: USE_CURL ON (OptionsPlayStation.cmake:271), ENABLE_STATIC_JSC defaults ON (:173), but USE_SKIA defaults OFF (WebKitFeatures.cmake:363, with Cairo at OptionsPlayStation.cmake:276-280), and it depends on libwpe (PlatformPlayStation.cmake:54-60, PasteboardLibWPE, PlatformKeyboardEventLibWPE). OptionsWin.cmake:91 USE_SKIA ON and :122 USE_CURL ON make WinCairo a second reference for Skia+curl, although it is WebKit2-only (ENABLE_WEBKIT ON at :30). — skeptic: JSCOnly (Source/cmake/OptionsJSCOnly.cmake): - :19 ENABLE_STATIC_JSC PUBLIC OFF - :35 DEFAULT_EVENT_LOOP_TYPE "Generic" - :39 set(ENABLE_WEBCORE OFF) - :41 set(ENABLE_WEBKIT OFF) - :89-94 ENABLE_JSC_GLIB_API forces EVENT_LOOP_TYPE "GLib" - :103-109 USE_GLIB / USE_GENERIC… | Base OptionsZeroG.cmake on OptionsPlayStation.cmake, borrowing the Skia setup from OptionsWin.cmake. Do not copy PlayStation's libwpe sources. |
| Bindings are installed when WebCore creates a frame's window object (the frame loader client's "window object cleared" callback). | CONFIRMED | loader/LocalFrameLoaderClient.h:292 `virtual void dispatchDidClearWindowObjectInWorld(DOMWrapperWorld&) = 0;`. It is dispatched by FrameLoader.cpp:5238-5244 only if `canExecuteScripts(...)` and `frame->windowProxy()->existingJSWindowProxy(world)`, and is called from bindings/js/ScriptController.cpp:358. It is per world. WebKitLegacy filters to mainThreadNormalWorldSingleton() (WebFrameLoaderClient.mm:1873). The JSGlobalContextRef comes from `toGlobalRef(frame->script().globalObject(world))` (WebKit/WebProcess/WebPage/WebFrame.cpp:1074), which needs <JavaScriptCore/APICast.h>, a private header exported at JavaScriptCore/CMakeLists.txt:567. | In ZGFrameLoaderClient::dispatchDidClearWindowObjectInWorld: check the world is the normal world, check frame.document()->securityOrigin() against the allowlist, then install JSObjectRefs with the JSC C API. It fires again on every navigation and for every subframe. |
| Storage: cookies use WebCore's SQLite cookie jar. | PARTIAL | CookieJarDB (platform/network/curl/CookieJarDB.h:72, `CookieJarDB(const String& databasePath)`, plus searchCookies/setCookie/deleteCookie) is in WebCore. However the WebCore::CookieJar base methods are only implemented for Cocoa: CookieJar.cpp:202-244 `#else // PLATFORM(COCOA)` makes them all ASSERT_NOT_REACHED. CookieStorageSession.h declares cookie APIs only under PLATFORM(COCOA). CookieJar's methods are virtual (CookieJar.h:63-82) and its constructor is protected (`CookieJar(Ref<StorageSessionProvider>&&)`). — skeptic: - Source/WebCore/platform/Curl.cmake:8-9,32-33 put CookieJarDB.cpp/.h and CookieUtil.cpp/.h into WebCore for curl ports; lines 57-60 link CURL::libcurl, LibPSL::LibPSL and OpenSSL::SSL. - Source/WebCore/platform/network/curl/CookieJarDB.h: - :29-30 includes SQLiteDatabase.h and SQLiteStatement.h - :62 searchCookies(...) - :64 setCookie(firstParty, url, cookie, Source, … | Write ZGCookieJar : WebCore::CookieJar, overriding cookies, setCookies, cookiesEnabled, cookieRequestHeaderFieldValue, getRawCookies, setRawCookie, deleteCookie, getCookiesAsync and setCookieAsync on top of CookieJarDB (port NetworkStorageSessionCurl.cpp). The curl loader must call the same store to inject Cookie headers and save Set-Cookie headers. |
| localStorage and IndexedDB are in-process implementations in the embedding layer, modelled on WebKitLegacy. | CONFIRMED | WebKitLegacy/Storage is portable C++ with almost no PLATFORM code: InProcessIDBServer.{h,cpp} (an IDBConnectionToServerDelegate and IDBConnectionToClientDelegate on a WorkQueue wrapping WebCore::IDBServer::IDBServer, with SQLiteIDBBackingStore and MemoryIDBBackingStore in WebCore/Modules/indexeddb/server), WebDatabaseProvider (DatabaseProvider::idbConnectionToServerForSession), WebStorageNamespaceProvider (a StorageNamespaceProvider), StorageNamespaceImpl, StorageAreaImpl, StorageAreaSync (SQLite), StorageSyncManager, StorageThread and StorageTracker. The only Apple-specific pieces are WebDatabaseProvider::indexedDatabaseDirectoryPath() in mac/Storage/WebDatabaseProvider.mm:32 and a SQLite3SPI include in StorageTracker.cpp:43. There are no 'LocalStorage*' files; the classes are named StorageArea*/StorageNamespace*. WebView.mm:1587-1589 wires them through pageConfiguration.databaseProvid… | Copy the 18 Storage files into ZeroG (Apple BSD license), replace indexedDatabaseDirectoryPath with the zg_context data directory, and initialise the StorageTracker (WebKitInitializeStorageIfNecessary equivalent). |
| Optional ephemeral mode with nothing written to disk. | CONFIRMED | WebDatabaseProvider.cpp:45 uses `sessionID.isEphemeral() ? InProcessIDBServer::create(sessionID) : create(sessionID, path)`. CookieJarDB.h:81 treats `":memory:"` as in-memory (used by NetworkStorageSessionCurl.cpp:93). StorageNamespaceProvider has createTransientLocalStorageNamespace. | Use PAL::SessionID::legacyPrivateSessionID() (or another ephemeral ID) for ephemeral contexts, as WebView.mm:1536 does. |
| The embedding layer implements resource loading (app:// on the main thread, http(s) via curl). | CONFIRMED | The seam is WebCore::PlatformStrategies (platform/PlatformStrategies.h): createLoaderStrategy, createPasteboardStrategy, createMediaStrategy and createBlobRegistry are pure, plus createPushStrategy if DECLARATIVE_WEB_PUSH is enabled; it is installed with setPlatformStrategies(). LoaderStrategy (loader/LoaderStrategy.h:64-117) has about 30 pure virtuals, including loadResource(LocalFrame&, CachedResource&, ResourceRequest&&, const ResourceLoaderOptions&, CompletionHandler<void(RefPtr<SubresourceLoader>&&)>&&), loadResourceSynchronously, startPingLoad/preconnectTo and the *Error factories. WebKitLegacy uses WebResourceLoadScheduler (WebCoreSupport/WebResourceLoadScheduler.cpp, 421 LOC), which calls SubresourceLoader::create and then loader->start(), i.e. ResourceHandle, unusable on curl. WebKit2's WebLoaderStrategy.cpp:157 also creates the SubresourceLoader, but feeds it via public Resour… | ZGLoaderStrategy creates the SubresourceLoader. For data: and blob: it calls loader->start(). For http(s) and app:// it starts its own CurlRequest or scheme handler and calls the ResourceLoader callbacks on the main thread. loadResourceSynchronously (sync XHR) and ping/keepalive (PingHandle in WebKitLegacy also uses ResourceHandle) need their own curl implementation. |

**Facts the doc misses**

- CookieJar is effectively abstract on non-Cocoa ports: every WebCore::CookieJar method is ASSERT_NOT_REACHED outside PLATFORM(COCOA) (WebCore/loader/CookieJar.cpp:202-244). CookieJar::create(StorageSessionProvider) on its own gives a page with no working cookies, so a subclass is required.
- NetworkStorageSession is no longer in WebCore. It moved to WebKit/Shared/NetworkStorageSession.h, with its curl part in WebKit/NetworkProcess/curl/NetworkStorageSessionCurl.cpp. WebCore keeps only the Cocoa-oriented CookieStorageSession (platform/network/CookieStorageSession.h:61), whose cookie API is #if PLATFORM(COCOA).
- Cookie header injection and Set-Cookie handling are not in WebCore's CurlRequest. They live in WebKit/NetworkProcess/curl/NetworkDataTaskCurl.cpp:541-565 (appendCookieHeader, handleCookieHeaders, blockCookies), alongside the redirect, auth and multipart handling ZeroG must re-implement.
- The four RefPtr members databaseProvider, pluginInfoProvider, storageNamespaceProvider and visitedLinkStore are typed optional in PageConfiguration but are dereferenced unconditionally in the Page constructor (WebCore/page/Page.cpp:450-455). Leaving one null crashes.
- The Empty* clients cannot serve as base classes. EmptyFrameLoaderClient (loader/EmptyFrameLoaderClient.h, 107 `final` methods, e.g. dispatchDidClearWindowObjectInWorld at :180 and createNetworkingContext at :192) and EmptyChromeClient (EmptyClients.h:60, 97 finals) are mostly final. Most other Empty* classes (EmptyEditorClient, EmptyBackForwardClient, EmptyStorageNamespaceProvider, EmptySocketProvider, etc.) are file-local in EmptyClients.cpp. Workable bootstrap: call pageConfigurationWithEmptyClients() (exported, EmptyClients.h:231), then move-assign your own objects over the public members (UniqueRef/Ref are movable). Write real clients by copying the Empty* code as a template.
- WebSocket is no longer in WebCore. SocketProvider::createWebSocketChannel and createWebTransportSession are pure (page/SocketProvider.h:46-47). The WebKit1 WebSocketChannel (hybi framing, 862 LOC) and SocketStreamHandle moved to WebKitLegacy/WebCoreSupport, where only a CFNetwork backend exists (SocketStreamHandleImplCFNet.cpp). WebCore still has WebSocketHandshake, WebSocketFrame, WebSocketDeflater and the curl transport CurlStream/CurlStreamScheduler (used by WebKit/NetworkProcess/curl/WebSocketTaskCurl.cpp). LegacySocketProvider.cpp shows how to delegate WebTransport to WebCore::emptySocketProvider().
- InspectorClient no longer exists; the class is InspectorBackendClient (inspector/InspectorBackendClient.h, 5 pure virtuals). WebKitLegacy also builds a LegacyWebPageInspectorController and a LegacyWebPageDebuggable for remote inspection (WebView.mm:1604-1610, WebCoreSupport/LegacyWebPage*). The dev-profile inspector needs equivalents of these.
- Process-global setup WebKitLegacy performs before the first Page (WebView.mm:1480-1527): WebPlatformStrategies::initializeIfNecessary() (setPlatformStrategies), initializeDOMWrapperHooks(), the storage tracker (WebKitInitializeStorageIfNecessary) and the log channels. MessagePortChannelProvider and WebLockRegistry fall back to in-process defaults (MessagePortChannelProviderImpl, LocalWebLockRegistry), so no code is needed for them.
- BlobRegistry must be supplied through PlatformStrategies::createBlobRegistry. The WebKitLegacy wrapper (WebPlatformStrategies.mm:86-112) forwards to an in-process WebCore::BlobRegistryImpl and can be copied as is. blob: loads then go through BlobResourceHandle via ResourceHandle's built-in constructor map, the one ResourceHandle path that still works on curl.
- MediaStrategy has one pure virtual, createAudioDestination (platform/MediaStrategy.h:60, under WEB_AUDIO); WebKitLegacy returns AudioDestination::create(options). PasteboardStrategy has 42 pure virtuals (platform/PasteboardStrategy.h). Non-Cocoa Pasteboard implementations exist only for Win, GTK, WPE and libwpe (platform/win/PasteboardWin.cpp, platform/libwpe/PasteboardLibWPE.cpp), so ZeroG needs its own Pasteboard.cpp for its platform.
- Rendering is driven by the embedder: ChromeClient::triggerRenderingUpdate() and attachRootGraphicsLayer() are pure (ChromeClient.h:519, :527), and the host must call Page::updateRendering() and finalizeRenderingUpdate() (Page.h:879-884) from its own frame tick, as WebKitLegacy does from a run-loop observer (WebView.mm:8884).
- ChromeClient::createPopupMenu and createSearchPopupMenu are pure (ChromeClient.h:641-642), so <select> needs a PopupMenu implementation. EditorClient needs a TextCheckerClient (9 pure virtuals).
- CacheStorageProvider::create() returns a DummyCacheStorageConnection that rejects every operation (page/CacheStorageProvider.h:36-58), and DummyStorageProvider makes navigator.storage persist/estimate/getDirectory (OPFS) fail. The Cache API, OPFS and storage estimates therefore do not work unless ZeroG implements them. The doc's Storage section does not mention this.
- DocumentSyncClient and FrameTreeSyncClient are generated sources (WebCore/CMakeLists.txt:2845-2864, DerivedSources). makeUniqueRef<WebCore::DocumentSyncClient>() is what both WebKitLegacy and EmptyClients pass.
- HistoryItemClient (history/HistoryItem.h:66, 2 pure) and BadgeClient are mandatory Ref constructor arguments. EmptyBadgeClient::create() is public (Modules/badge/EmptyBadgeClient.h). EmptyHistoryItemClient is file-local, so ZeroG needs its own trivial copy (LegacyHistoryItemClient.mm is 44 LOC).
- LocalFrameLoaderClient::createNetworkingContext() is pure and returns a FrameNetworkingContext (a NetworkingContext, which is a StorageSessionProvider). On non-Cocoa, non-Win builds it only needs shouldClearReferrerOnHTTPSToHTTPRedirect() plus storageSession() (platform/network/NetworkingContext.h:43-66).

<details><summary>Concrete starting material</summary>

```text
PageConfiguration constructor (WebCore/page/PageConfiguration.h:122-154), argument by argument, with the WebCore default and the WebKitLegacy choice (WebView.mm:1534-1590):
1. std::optional<PageIdentifier>: PageIdentifier::generate()
2. PAL::SessionID: defaultSessionID() or legacyPrivateSessionID() (ephemeral)
3. UniqueRef<EditorClient>: Empty = EmptyEditorClient (file-local); Legacy = WebEditorClient.mm. ZeroG writes ZGEditorClient (88 pure virtuals + TextCheckerClient).
4. Ref<SocketProvider>: Empty = emptySocketProvider() (exported, no WebSocket); Legacy = LegacySocketProvider, which wraps WebSocketChannel over SocketStreamHandle (CFNet). ZeroG: ZGSocketProvider + ported WebSocketChannel + SocketStreamHandleImplCurl over CurlStream.
5. UniqueRef<WebRTCProvider>: WebRTCProvider::create() for both.
6. Ref<CacheStorageProvider>: CacheStorageProvider::create() (dummy) for both.
7. Ref<UserContentProvider>: Empty = EmptyUserContentProvider; Legacy = WebViewGroup::userContentController(), a WebCore::UserContentController (concrete, page/UserContentController.h:34). ZeroG: UserContentController::create().
8. Ref<BackForwardClient>: Empty = EmptyBackForwardClient; Legacy = BackForwardList (mac/History/BackForwardList.mm, 261 LOC). ZeroG writes ZGBackForwardList (9 pure virtuals).
9. Ref<CookieJar>: Empty = CookieJar::create(EmptyStorageSessionProvider); Legacy = CookieJar::create(PageStorageSessionProvider), Cocoa only. ZeroG writes ZGCookieJar : CookieJar over CookieJarDB.
10. UniqueRef<ProgressTrackerClient>: Empty = EmptyProgressTrackerClient; Legacy = WebProgressTrackerClient.
11. MainFrameCreationParameters: LocalMainFrameCreationParameters{ [](LocalFrame&, FrameLoader& fl){ return makeUniqueRefWithoutRefCountedCheck<ZGFrameLoaderClient>(fl); }, SandboxFlags{}, ReferrerPolicy::EmptyString }
12. FrameIdentifier: generateFrameIdentifier()
13. RefPtr<Frame> opener: nullptr
14. UniqueRef<SpeechRecognitionProvider>: makeUniqueRef<DummySpeechRecognitionProvider>() (page/DummySpeechRecognitionProvider.h)
15. Ref<BroadcastChannelRegistry>: Empty = EmptyBroadcastChannelRegistry; Legacy = WebBroadcastChannelRegistry (portable, 86 LOC, copy it).
16. UniqueRef<StorageProvider>: makeUniqueRef<DummyStorageProvider>() (Modules/storage/DummyStorageProvider.h)
17. Ref<ModelPlayerProvider>: DummyModelPlayerProvider::create()
18. Ref<BadgeClient>: EmptyBadgeClient::create()
19. Ref<HistoryItemClient>: own trivial class (Legacy = LegacyHistoryItemClient)
20. [CONTEXT_MENUS] UniqueRef<ContextMenuClient>: EmptyContextMenuClient / WebContextMenuClient
21. [APPLE_PAY] Ref<PaymentCoordinatorClient>: disable APPLE_PAY
22. UniqueRef<ChromeClient>: EmptyChromeClient (EmptyClients.h:60) / WebChromeClient. ZeroG writes ZGChromeClient (90 pure virtuals).
23. UniqueRef<CryptoClient>: EmptyCryptoClient (EmptyClients.h:233); base CryptoClient defaults already return nullopt.
24. UniqueRef<DocumentSyncClient>: makeUniqueRef<DocumentSyncClient>()
25. [WEB_AUTHN] Ref<CredentialRequestCoordinatorClient>: disable WEB_AUTHN, or use Dummy/EmptyCredentialRequestCoordinatorClient.

Members to set after construction:
- Mandatory (dereferenced in Page.cpp:450-455):
  - databaseProvider = WebDatabaseProvider (InProcessIDBServer)
  - pluginInfoProvider = own empty provider (3 pure virtuals)
  - storageNamespaceProvider = WebStorageNamespaceProvider::create(localStorageDir)
  - visitedLinkStore = own store (2 pure virtuals)
- Optional (unique_ptr):
  - dragClient
  - inspectorBackendClient
  - validationMessageClient
  - alternativeTextClient
  - diagnosticLoggingClient (Page falls back to emptyDiagnosticLoggingClient(), Page.cpp:1768)
  - performanceLoggingClient
  - attachmentElementClient
  - speechSynthesisClient

PlatformStrategies skeleton (port of WebKitLegacy/mac/WebCoreSupport/WebPlatformStrategies.{h,mm}):
```cpp
class ZGPlatformStrategies final : public WebCore::PlatformStrategies {
  WebCore::LoaderStrategy* createLoaderStrategy() override { return new ZGLoaderStrategy; }
  WebCore::PasteboardStrategy* createPasteboardStrategy() override; // 42 pure virtuals
  WebCore::MediaStrategy* createMediaStrategy() override; // createAudioDestination -> AudioDestination::create(opts)
  WebCore::BlobRegistry* createBlobRegistry() override; // forward to WebCore::BlobRegistryImpl (copy WebBlobRegistry, mm:86-112)
};
// once, before the first Page: WebCore::setPlatformStrategies(new ZGPlatformStrategies);
```

Page bring-up sequence (WebView.mm:1533-1650, WebFrame.mm:355-363):
1. auto page = Page::create(WTF::move(cfg));
2. localMainFrame = dynamicDowncast<LocalFrame>(page->mainFrame());
3. localMainFrame->init();
4. call localMainFrame->createView(size, bg, fixedLayoutSize, false) from the client's transitionToCommittedForNewPage, as WebLocalFrameLoaderClient.cpp:1774 does.
5. Each tick: page->updateRendering(); page->finalizeRenderingUpdate(...).

Window-object-cleared hook:
```cpp
#include <JavaScriptCore/APICast.h>
void ZGFrameLoaderClient::dispatchDidClearWindowObjectInWorld(WebCore::DOMWrapperWorld& world) {
  if (&world != &WebCore::mainThreadNormalWorldSingleton()) return;
  auto& frame = m_localFrame; // check frame.document()->securityOrigin() against the allowlist
  JSGlobalContextRef ctx = toGlobalRef(frame.script().globalObject(world));
  // install App.* via JSObjectMakeFunctionWithCallback / JSObjectSetProperty
}
```

Files worth copying almost verbatim (portable C++, Apple BSD license):
- Source/WebKitLegacy/Storage/: InProcessIDBServer, StorageAreaImpl, StorageAreaSync, StorageNamespaceImpl, StorageSyncManager, StorageThread, StorageTracker, StorageTrackerClient.h, WebDatabaseProvider, WebStorageNamespaceProvider (18 files, 3433 LOC). Replace mac/Storage/WebDatabaseProvider.mm::indexedDatabaseDirectoryPath.
- Source/WebKitLegacy/WebCoreSupport/: WebBroadcastChannelRegistry.{h,cpp}, WebSocketChannel.{h,cpp}, SocketStreamHandle.{h,cpp}, SocketStreamHandleImpl.{h,cpp}, SocketStreamHandleClient.h, LegacySocketProvider.{h,cpp} (the transport must be rewritten over WebCore CurlStream).
- To port, not copy: WebKit/NetworkProcess/curl/NetworkDataTaskCurl.cpp (curl loader logic), NetworkStorageSessionCurl.cpp (cookies over CookieJarDB), WebSocketTaskCurl.cpp; WebKit/WebProcess/Network/WebLoaderStrategy.cpp and WebResourceLoader.cpp (how to feed a SubresourceLoader without ResourceHandle).
- Client references in plain C++: WebKit/WebProcess/WebCoreSupport/{WebChromeClient, WebEditorClient, WebLocalFrameLoaderClient, WebFrameLoaderClient, WebContextMenuClient, WebDragClient, WebProgressTrackerClient, WebPopupMenu, WebSearchPopupMenu}.cpp.

Estimated size of the minimum embedding layer: about 25-30 classes, about 55-70 source files (.h + .cpp), roughly 15-25k LOC.
- Hand-written clients (~22 files, ~8-10k LOC): ChromeClient, EditorClient + TextChecker, LocalFrameLoaderClient, ContextMenuClient, DragClient, ProgressTrackerClient, InspectorBackendClient (dev only), BackForwardList, HistoryItemClient, VisitedLinkStore, PluginInfoProvider, FrameNetworkingContext, PopupMenu, SearchPopupMenu.
- Strategies and loader (~10 files, ~3-5k LOC): PlatformStrategies, LoaderStrategy, curl network task, app:// scheme task, BlobRegistry wrapper, Pasteboard strategy.
- Cookies (~4 files, ~1k LOC): CookieJar subclass, StorageSessionProvider.
- WebSocket (~8 files, ~2k LOC): SocketProvider, WebSocketChannel, SocketStreamHandle over curl.
- Storage (18 copied files, ~3.4k LOC).
- BroadcastChannelRegistry (2 files).
- Page/View glue and JS bindings (~6-8 files).

Useful checks:
- grep -c "= 0;" Source/WebCore/loader/LocalFrameLoaderClient.h (103)
- grep -c "= 0;" Source/WebCore/page/ChromeClient.h (90)
- grep -c "= 0;" Source/WebCore/page/EditorClient.h (88)
```

REQUIRED)`) on any non-WIN32, non-PlayStation target, and links EGL unconditionally (:856-867). — skeptic: Source/WebCore/platform/graphics/skia/PlatformDisplaySkia.cpp:370-382 (guard `PLATFO… | Expect to patch WebCore's Skia code and Skia's CMakeLists, not just add GPU contexts. **Facts the doc misses**

- Skia's bundled third_party directories (freetype2, harfbuzz, icu, libpng, libjpeg-turbo, libwebp, zlib, expat, brotli) hold only BUILD.gn stubs and roll scripts, no sources (Source/ThirdParty/skia/third_party/*). Every dependency must be built and provided as CMake imported targets: Freetype::Freetype, Fontconfig::Fontconfig, JPEG::JPEG, PNG::PNG, WebP::mux, HarfBuzz::HarfBuzz, HarfBuzz::ICU, ICU::uc/i18n/data, plus EGL.
- The Skia CMakeLists.txt hard-requires Fontconfig and FreeType for any non-WIN32 and non-PLAYSTATION target (lines 4-7, 845-852). That includes macOS, which would need Fontconfig and EGL, with no CoreText or Metal sources. A ZeroG patch to this file is unavoidable on Linux and macOS.
- Skia is compiled with SK_ASSUME_GL_ES=1 (CMakeLists.txt:1084). Only GLES contexts are supported: EGL GLES on Linux, ANGLE on Windows and macOS. Desktop GL, GLX and WGL are not wired.
- PlatformDisplaySkia.cpp:349 creates `thread_local SkExecutor::MakeFIFOThreadPool(2)`, so every Skia GL context spawns 2 unnamed Skia worker threads. That matters for the doc's thread naming and thread count goals.
- PlatformDisplaySkia defaults MSAA to 0 except on GTK/WPE (lines 67-80). It reads the env vars WEBKIT_SKIA_MSAA_SAMPLE_COUNT (line 209) and getenv elsewhere, which conflicts with 'config via zg_runtime_config, not env vars'.
- SkiaGLContext is thread_local (PlatformDisplaySkia.cpp:96) and every accelerated path calls PlatformDisplay::sharedDisplay().skiaGLContext()->makeContextCurrent(). Rendering into an app-provided GL context (texture mode) means injecting it with PlatformDisplay::setSharedDisplay(...) and setSkiaGLContextForCurrentThread(std::unique_ptr<GLContext>), wrapping an existing EGLContext in a WebCore GLContext. Sharing an app's device requires EGL-level context sharing, not just a GrDirectContext.
- On PLATFORM(WIN), PlatformDisplay::sharedDisplay() is a hard-coded function-static ANGLE display, and setSharedDisplay/destroySharedDisplay do not exist (PlatformDisplay.cpp:49-55, PlatformDisplay.h:64-68). An app-supplied D3D11 device cannot be injected without patching. A new PLATFORM avoids this but loses Win-only branches.
- PlayStation with USE_COORDINATED_GRAPHICS explicitly disables Skia GL ('The PlayStation OpenGL implementation does not dispatch to the context bound to the current thread', PlatformDisplaySkia.cpp:379-381). PlayStation is therefore not a working reference for GPU Skia, only for CPU Skia plus CMake structure.
- WebKit's Win port compositing (GraphicsLayerWC, WCScene, RemoteWCLayerTreeHost) is WebKit2 GPU-process IPC code (WebKit/WebProcess/WebPage/wc, WebKit/GPUProcess/graphics/wc) and cannot be reused in single-process. GraphicsLayerTextureMapper (WebCore, only when NOT USE_COORDINATED_GRAPHICS) is the reusable in-process compositor.
- HarfBuzz must come with the ICU component (hb-icu): Skia.cmake links HarfBuzz::ICU and ComplexTextControllerSkia.cpp uses hb_icu_script_to_script. This contradicts the doc's 'HarfBuzz without ICU'.
- SkPDF is always compiled into Skia (CMakeLists.txt:611-631) and pulls in zlib (SkDeflate.cpp:21). Only GTK printing uses it.
- SkFontMgr_Custom has no per-character fallback (onMatchFamilyStyleCharacter returns nullptr), no generic-family aliasing, and an immutable family list. Runtime font registration needs a custom SkFontMgr subclass.
- TextureMapper, BitmapTexture and SkiaGPUAtlas are GL-only. Any compositor reuse on macOS implies ANGLE (Metal backend) as the GL implementation.
- FontCache::lastResortFallbackFont uses 'Times New Roman' on PLATFORM(WIN) and 'serif' elsewhere (FontCacheSkia.cpp:182-186). With a custom font manager, 'serif' must resolve, or the code falls back to SkTypeface::MakeEmpty() and renders no glyphs.

- EGL sources every Skia port compiles (copy from PlatformPlayStation.cmake / PlatformWin.cmake): platform/graphics/PlatformDisplay.cpp, egl/BitmapTexture.cpp, egl/BitmapTexturePool.cpp, egl/GLContext.cpp, egl/GLContextWrapper.cpp, egl/GLDisplay.cpp, egl/GLFence.cpp, egl/GLFenceEGL.cpp, egl/GLFenceGL.cpp, plus egl/GraphicsContextGLEGL.cpp if WebGL is on.
- Compositor v2 candidate: include(platform/TextureMapper.cmake) with USE_TEXTURE_MAPPER ON and USE_COORDINATED_GRAPHICS OFF, which compiles texmap/GraphicsLayerTextureMapper.cpp.

Minimal Options sketch (OptionsZeroG.cmake):
```
WEBKIT_OPTION_DEFAULT_PORT_VALUE(USE_SKIA PRIVATE ON)
WEBKIT_OPTION_DEFAULT_PORT_VALUE(USE_SKIA_ENCODERS PRIVATE OFF)  # avoids WebP mux in Skia
WEBKIT_OPTION_DEFAULT_PORT_VALUE(ENABLE_WEBGL PRIVATE OFF)
SET_AND_EXPOSE_TO_BUILD(USE_HARFBUZZ ON)
SET_AND_EXPOSE_TO_BUILD(USE_CAIRO OFF)
# Windows only: set(USE_ANGLE_EGL ON) -> builds ThirdParty/ANGLE without WebGL; force EGL/GLESv2 STATIC
find_package(ICU 70.1 REQUIRED COMPONENTS data i18n uc)
find_package(HarfBuzz REQUIRED COMPONENTS ICU)
find_package(Freetype 2.10.2 REQUIRED)   # build with brotli -> HAVE_WOFF_SUPPORT, drop libwoff2
find_package(PNG REQUIRED)
find_package(JPEG REQUIRED)               # Skia links these unconditionally
```

Disabling compositing (v1): Settings::setAcceleratedCompositingEnabled(false), and/or ChromeClient::allowedCompositingTriggers() returning 0. Then paint with:
```
GraphicsContextSkia ctx(*surface->getCanvas(), RenderingMode::Unaccelerated /*or Accelerated*/, RenderingPurpose::DOM);
frameView->paint(ctx, dirtyRect);
```
Model on DrawingAreaWC::sendUpdateNonAC (Source/WebKit/WebProcess/WebPage/wc/DrawingAreaWC.cpp:337-375).

Windows device and texture sharing via ANGLE (not used upstream):
- eglCreateDeviceANGLE(EGL_D3D11_DEVICE_ANGLE, appDevice, nullptr), then eglGetPlatformDisplayEXT(EGL_PLATFORM_DEVICE_EXT, device, ...)
- eglCreatePbufferFromClientBuffer(dpy, EGL_D3D_TEXTURE_ANGLE, tex, ...) — see Source/ThirdParty/ANGLE/include/EGL/eglext_angle.h:25-27 and :209.

rAF pacing: implement DisplayRefreshMonitorFactory and return it from ChromeClient::displayRefreshMonitorFactory() (ChromeClient.h:484).
```

</details>

**Facts the doc misses**

- CurlSSLHandle::platformInitialize() has implementations only for Windows (platform/network/win/CurlSSLHandleWin.cpp) and PlayStation (platform/network/playstation/CurlSSLHandlePlayStation.cpp). ZeroG must write Linux and macOS versions or the link fails (declared at CurlSSLHandle.h:98).
- NetworkStateNotifier needs per-platform updateStateWithoutNotifying()/startObserving(). Only Win, GLib and PlayStation exist (platform/network/win, glib, playstation; NetworkStateNotifier.h:66-90). ZeroG needs its own, e.g. copy the PlayStation notImplemented() stubs, or iphlpapi on Windows and netlink or SCNetworkReachability elsewhere.
- ResourceHandle is a stub for USE(CURL) (ResourceHandle.cpp:312-360 ASSERT_NOT_REACHED), and no ResourceHandleCurl exists. ResourceLoader is still a ResourceHandleClient (ResourceLoader.h:69), so ZeroG can either revive a ResourceHandleCurl on top of CurlRequest or drive ResourceLoader from a custom LoaderStrategy, the way WebKit2's WebResourceLoader does.
- NetworkStorageSession is no longer in WebCore. It lives in Source/WebKit/Shared/NetworkStorageSession.h with its curl impl in Source/WebKit/NetworkProcess/curl/NetworkStorageSessionCurl.cpp. WebCore only has CookieStorageSession, whose cookie API exists only on Cocoa (CookieStorageSession.h). The base WebCore::CookieJar is all ASSERT_NOT_REACHED on non-Cocoa (loader/CookieJar.cpp:202-244).
- Single-process WebSocketChannel and SocketStreamHandle moved to Source/WebKitLegacy/WebCoreSupport (WebSocketChannel.cpp, SocketStreamHandleImpl*.cpp, LegacySocketProvider.cpp). The only backend there is CFNetwork, and WebKitLegacy only builds for Cocoa. The WebCore protocol pieces (WebSocketHandshake/Frame/Deflater, ThreadableWebSocketChannel, WorkerThreadableWebSocketChannel) are still in WebCore.
- SocketProvider also requires createWebTransportSession(). LegacySocketProvider delegates it to WebCore::emptySocketProvider() (LegacySocketProvider.cpp).
- CurlStream does its connect and TLS handshake with a blocking curl_easy_perform inside the shared curlStreamThread (CurlStream.cpp:40-64), and uses select() with fd_set (CurlStreamScheduler.cpp:157-190). Many concurrent or slow WebSocket connects serialize. FD_SETSIZE is 64 by default on Windows.
- The curl port has no HSTS support: no HSTS or CURLOPT_HSTS anywhere in platform/network/curl or WebKit/NetworkProcess/curl.
- Alt-Svc and HTTP/3 stay off unless CurlContext::setAlternativeServicesStorageFile() is called (CurlContext.cpp:558-572). Upstream only WebKit2's NetworkStorageSessionCurl.cpp:97 calls it.
- On Windows, CurlHandle::enableSSL uses only CURLSSLOPT_NATIVE_CA and ignores CurlSSLHandle's CA path/blob (CurlContext.cpp:346-353), so an embedded CA bundle does not work on Windows without a patch.
- No sync mode in CurlRequest: all client callbacks go through ensureOnMainThread (CurlRequest.cpp:174-177). LoaderStrategy::loadResourceSynchronously (sync XHR) therefore needs a nested run-loop or a dedicated path to avoid deadlock.
- The curl code needs minimum curl versions: curl_multi_poll/curl_multi_wakeup (CurlContext.cpp:274,279), CURLOPT_PROTOCOLS_STR (L555, curl >= 7.85), CURLOPT_CAINFO_BLOB (L597). OptionsWin.cmake:44 requires CURL 7.87.0, OptionsPlayStation.cmake:105 requires 7.85.0. CurlContext constructor RELEASE_ASSERTs CURL_VERSION_LARGEFILE (CurlContext.cpp:130).
- Network tuning reads environment variables at CurlContext construction (WEBKIT_CURL_DNS_CACHE_TIMEOUT, WEBKIT_CURL_CONNECT_TIMEOUT, WEBKIT_CURL_MAXCONNECTS, WEBKIT_CURL_MAX_TOTAL_CONNECTIONS, WEBKIT_CURL_MAX_HOST_CONNECTIONS; Debug: DEBUG_CURL, CURL_LOG_FILE). Host apps inherit this behavior.
- CurlProxySettings and proxy configuration are applied through CurlContext::setProxySettings (NetworkStorageSessionCurl.cpp:252-254). ZeroG must expose this itself.
- Non-special custom schemes get opaque origins unless registerURLSchemeAsHandledBySchemeHandler is called (SecurityOriginData.cpp:193-231). This must be part of app:// and zg_context_register_scheme registration.
- A BoringSSL source tree with a CMakeLists.txt already ships in the checkout at Source/ThirdParty/libwebrtc/Source/third_party/boringssl/src (rev 2ea3200c3a3ec176613ab7a0b9a4ffc6db846120, CPE 0.20260413.0; targets crypto, ssl, decrepit). It can be vendored, but its static target names 'crypto'/'ssl' are generic. If the host app links its own OpenSSL, the OpenSSL symbols collide, so consider BORINGSSL_PREFIX symbol prefixing.
- USE_BORINGSSL is detected in OptionsWin.cmake:138 and OptionsPlayStation.cmake:306 but no C++ code uses it, so no BoringSSL-specific code paths exist. The only implicit ones are __has_include(<openssl/hkdf.h>) in CryptoAlgorithmHKDFOpenSSL.cpp:34 and the OPENSSL_VERSION_NUMBER < 3.0 branches (BoringSSL reports 0x1010107f).
- GTK/WPE's OpenSSL backend path requires OpenSSL >= 3.0 and defines OPENSSL_API_COMPAT=0x10100000L because the backend uses deprecated APIs (PlatformGTK.cmake:11-12, bug 245146). If ZeroG uses OpenSSL 3 as the alternative, it needs the same define.
- WebSocket permessage-deflate needs zlib directly in WebCore (WebSocketDeflater.cpp:39), not only through curl.
- DNSResolveQueueCurl is entirely notImplemented(), so DNS prefetch and preconnect hints do nothing (DNSResolveQueueCurl.cpp).

<details><summary>Concrete starting material</summary>

```text
Port CMake skeleton for the network and crypto area (WebCore/PlatformZeroG.cmake):
  include(platform/Curl.cmake)      # 21 curl sources, links CURL::libcurl LibPSL::LibPSL OpenSSL::SSL
  include(platform/OpenSSL.cmake)   # 21 crypto/openssl sources (no libs: add OpenSSL::Crypto)
  list(APPEND WebCore_LIBRARIES OpenSSL::Crypto ZLIB::ZLIB SQLite3::SQLite3)
  list(APPEND WebCore_SOURCES
    platform/network/zerog/CurlSSLHandleZeroG.cpp        # NEW: platformInitialize() per OS (CA path / blob, cipher list like CurlSSLHandleWin.cpp)
    platform/network/zerog/NetworkStateNotifierZeroG.cpp # NEW (or reuse win/NetworkStateNotifierWin.cpp on Windows)
  )
  if (WIN32) list(APPEND WebCore_LIBRARIES crypt32 iphlpapi ws2_32) endif()
PAL/pal/PlatformZeroG.cmake: list(APPEND PAL_SOURCES crypto/openssl/CryptoDigestOpenSSL.cpp)  list(APPEND PAL_LIBRARIES OpenSSL::Crypto)
Options: SET_AND_EXPOSE_TO_BUILD(USE_CURL ON) SET_AND_EXPOSE_TO_BUILD(USE_OPENSSL ON); WEBKIT_CHECK_HAVE_SYMBOL(USE_BORINGSSL OPENSSL_IS_BORINGSSL openssl/ssl.h) (copy from OptionsWin.cmake:134-139). Windows static: add_definitions(-DCURL_STATICLIB -DPSL_STATIC).

Embedding-layer classes ZeroG must write (with upstream templates):
- ZGLoaderStrategy : WebCore::LoaderStrategy (loader/LoaderStrategy.h). Model it on WebKit/WebProcess/Network/WebLoaderStrategy.cpp plus NetworkDataTaskCurl.cpp (redirects L259-418, auth L420-540, cookies L541-590). Its loader is a CurlRequestClient (CurlRequestClient.h) and handles app:// directly.
- ZGCookieJar : WebCore::CookieJar over WebCore::CookieJarDB (port NetworkStorageSessionCurl.cpp, 264 lines; ephemeral = ":memory:").
- ZGSocketProvider : WebCore::SocketProvider plus a ZGWebSocketChannel : ThreadableWebSocketChannel, CurlStream::Client. Port WebKitLegacy/WebCoreSupport/WebSocketChannel.cpp (862 lines), or WebKit/NetworkProcess/curl/WebSocketTaskCurl.cpp (510 lines) with the NetworkSocketChannel IPC removed. Use CurlContext::singleton().streamScheduler().createStream(url, client, ServerTrustEvaluation::Enable, LocalhostAlias::Disable).
- Startup: CurlContext::singleton().setAlternativeServicesStorageFile(dataDir+"/altsvc.txt"); sslHandle().setCACertPath(...) or setCACertData(...); setProxySettings(...).

Scheme registration for app:// (and each zg_context_register_scheme):
  LegacySchemeRegistry::registerURLSchemeAsHandledBySchemeHandler("app"_s); // else opaque origin (SecurityOriginData.cpp:231)
  LegacySchemeRegistry::registerURLSchemeAsSecure("app"_s);
  LegacySchemeRegistry::registerURLSchemeAsCORSEnabled("app"_s);
  // optional: registerURLSchemeAsBypassingContentSecurityPolicy, registerURLSchemeAsCachePartitioned

curl build flags (CMake): -DCURL_USE_OPENSSL=ON (pointing at BoringSSL) -DUSE_NGHTTP2=ON -DCURL_BROTLI=ON -DCURL_ZSTD=ON(if kept) -DUSE_LIBIDN2=OFF -DUSE_WIN32_IDN=OFF -DENABLE_THREADED_RESOLVER=ON -DENABLE_ARES=OFF -DCURL_USE_LIBPSL=OFF (WebKit uses libpsl itself) -DCURL_DISABLE_FTP/LDAP/SMTP/...=ON. Keep FILE or serve file:// in ZeroG (WebKit sets CURLOPT_PROTOCOLS_STR "file,http,https"). The ALTSVC feature must stay on for HTTP/3. WebSockets in curl are not required.

ICU converter keep-list (TextCodecICU.cpp:83-104): ISO-8859-2,4,5,10,13,14,15,16; KOI8-R; macintosh; windows-1250,1251,1254,1256,1258; x-mac-cyrillic, x-mac-greek, x-mac-centraleurroman, x-mac-turkish; EUC-TW; plus cnvalias. Debug builds also need EUC-JP, Big5, windows-949 and gb18030 (EncodingTables.cpp ASSERT self-checks).

Vendored BoringSSL available: Source/ThirdParty/libwebrtc/Source/third_party/boringssl/src (CMakeLists.txt, targets crypto/ssl).
```

</details>
